\|x_{j}\|_{\varpi,j}:=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{j}(n,\varpi)x_{j}\|e^{-\lambda_{j}n-\epsilon|n|},\qquad\forall x_{j}\in X_{j}.

It satisfies that

\|x_{j}\|\leq\|x_{j}\|_{\varpi,j}\leq\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\|x_{j}\|,

where
\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega):=C_{\lambda}K_{\epsilon}(\omega)>0
, implying

\displaystyle\|x_{j}\|\leq\|x_{j}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}\leq\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)\|x_{j}\|,\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}.

(7.9)

Then,
[
6
, Lemma 1]
tells that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{j}(n,\varpi)x_{j}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}\leq e^{(\lambda_{j}-\epsilon)n}\|x_{j}\|_{\varpi,j},\qquad\forall n<0,\\
&\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{j}(n,\varpi)x_{j}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}\leq e^{(\lambda_{j}+\epsilon)n}\|x_{j}\|_{\varpi,j},\qquad\forall n\geq 0,\end{split}

(7.10)

for all
x_{j}\in X_{j}
. The necessity of using the Lyapunov norms will be illustrated at the end of this section.

Next, define

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{b,n}_{i,j}:=\bigg\{h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in C^{0}(X_{i},X_{j}):h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,{{0}})={{0}},\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{{{0}}\}}\frac{\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{b}}<\infty\bigg\},

with a real
b\in\mathbb{R}
for
i=1,...,\tau
and
j=1,...,{p}
, which is a Banach space equipped with the norm

\displaystyle\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{b,n}_{i,j}}:=\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{{{0}}\}}\frac{\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{b}}.

Remind that we assume that
\alpha\in(0,1]
in this section. Let
\beta>0
be the constant obtained in Theorem
4.1

and assume that

\displaystyle 0<\beta\leq\alpha

(7.11)

without loss of generality because, otherwise, we use
\alpha
instead of
\beta
.
Let
\varsigma>0
be given in (
5.30
). Then,

\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}:=\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}\cap\Upsilon^{\varsigma,n}_{i,j}

is a Banach space equipped with the norm

\displaystyle\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}}:=\max\big\{\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}},~\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\varsigma,n}_{i,j}}\big\}.

(7.12)

This enables us to define

\Upsilon^{n}_{i}:=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{{p}}\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j},

which is also a Banach space equipped with the norm

\|h_{i}\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}:=\max_{j=1,...,{p}}\|h_{i,j}\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}}.

We have the following lemma concerning the invertibility of
T_{i,j}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

For any
\{{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}

(
i=1,...,\tau
,
j=1,...{p}
)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}}\}<\infty,

(7.13)

the linear operator
T_{i,j}
defined by
(
7.7
)
has an inverse
T_{i,j}^{-1}
such that

T_{i,j}^{-1}{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|T_{i,j}^{-1}{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}}\}\leq C_{\lambda}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}}\}

(7.14)

for
i=1,...,\tau
and
j=1,...,p
, where
C_{\lambda}>0
is a constant.

This lemma will be proved after the completion of this proof.
Recalling that
T_{i}:=\sum_{j=1}^{{p}}T_{i,j}
and

\|h_{i}\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}:=\max_{j=1,...,{p}}\|h_{i,j}\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}},

it is clear that
T_{i}^{-1}{h}_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,{{0}})={{0}}
and we obtain from (
7.14
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|T_{i}^{-1}{h}_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\}\leq C_{\lambda}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{h}_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\}

(7.15)

for any
\{{h}_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset\Upsilon^{n}_{i}
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{h}_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\}<\infty.

Step 2
.
The contraction of
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
.

Next, we show that the nonlinear operator
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
given in (
7.8
) is contractive. Since

D\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})=\partial_{x_{u}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})+\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})

and

\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{\kappa}(n,\varpi)\,\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}\in X_{\kappa}\subset X_{s},

we see from (
7.8
) that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})

\displaystyle=-\bar{\bf\Lambda}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi)D\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\{\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{\kappa}(n,\varpi)\,\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}+\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\}

\displaystyle=-\bar{\bf\Lambda}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi)\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{\kappa}(n,\varpi)\,\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}

\displaystyle\quad-\bar{\bf\Lambda}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi)D\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}).

(7.16)

In order to show that

{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i},

we see easily that
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)=0
. Moreover, we
notice that
D(\pi_{s}\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)={\bf 0}
because

\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)=\varphi_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\circ\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot),

as known from (
5.25
),
and

D(\pi_{s}\varphi_{i}^{-1})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)=\frac{d\{x_{s}-\pi_{s}\gamma_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\}}{d{x_{i}}}\Big|_{x_{i}=0}={\bf 0}

by the second equality of (
5.20
) and by the fact that
x_{i}\in X_{u}
for
i=1,...,\tau
. It follows that

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)

\displaystyle=\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x_{u}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)D(\pi_{u}\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)+\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)D(\pi_{s}\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)

\displaystyle=\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)D(\pi_{s}\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)={\bf 0}

(7.17)

since

\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x_{u}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)={\bf 0}

by the third equality of (
5.11
).

On the other hand, notice that

\displaystyle\|D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|

\displaystyle=\|D\varphi_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))D\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|\leq 2(1+C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)\|x_{i}\|^{\beta})

\displaystyle\leq 2(1+C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega))\quad{\rm when}~\|x_{i}\|\leq 1,

and that

\displaystyle\|D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})-D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|

\displaystyle=\|D\varphi_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))D\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})-D\varphi_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)D\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|D\varphi_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))-D\varphi_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|\,\|D\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|D\varphi_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|\,\|D\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})-D\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{\epsilon,\gamma}(\theta^{n}\omega)\|\varphi_{i,*}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|^{\beta}+2C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)\|x_{i}\|^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\{L_{\epsilon,\gamma}(\theta^{n}\omega)(1+C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)\|x_{i}\|^{\beta})+2C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)\}\|x_{i}\|^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq L_{5\epsilon,\gamma}(\theta^{n}\omega)\|x_{i}\|^{\beta}\quad{\rm when}~\|x_{i}\|\leq 1

due to (
5.20
) and (
5.24
), where
L_{5\epsilon,\gamma}(\omega):=L_{\epsilon,\gamma}(\omega)(1+C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\omega))+2C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\omega)>0
.
Then, we see that when
0<\|x_{i}\|\leq 1

\displaystyle\frac{\|\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))-\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0))\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sup_{c\in(0,1)}\|\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i}))D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i})\|}{\|cx_{i}\|^{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sup_{c\in(0,1)}\|\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i}))D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i})-\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|}{\|cx_{i}\|^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sup_{c\in(0,1)}\|\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i}))-\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|\,\|D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|}{\|cx_{i}\|^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sup_{c\in(0,1)}\|\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i}))\|\,\|D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i})-D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)\|}{\|cx_{i}\|^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{L}_{\epsilon,\delta}(\theta^{n}\omega)\frac{\sup_{c\in(0,1)}\|\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i})\|^{\beta}}{\|cx_{i}\|^{\beta}}+\bar{M}_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)L_{5\epsilon,\gamma}(\theta^{n}\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\bar{L}_{\epsilon,\delta}(\theta^{n}\omega)\{2(1+C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega))\}^{\beta}+\bar{M}_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)L_{5\epsilon,\gamma}(\theta^{n}\omega)\leq e^{6\epsilon|n|}\bar{C}_{6\epsilon}(\omega)

(7.18)

by (
5.15
), (
7.17
) and (
7.11
), where

\bar{C}_{6\epsilon}(\omega):=\bar{L}_{\epsilon,\delta}(\omega)\{2(1+C^{*}_{4\epsilon}(\omega))\}^{\beta}+\bar{M}_{\epsilon}(\omega)L_{5\epsilon,\gamma}(\omega)
,
and that when
\|x_{i}\|>1

\frac{\|\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{1+\beta}}\leq\|\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|\leq C_{\lambda}\delta(\theta^{n}\omega)\leq e^{6\epsilon|n|}\bar{C}_{6\epsilon}(\omega)

due to the first inequality of (
5.15
). It means that

\displaystyle e^{-6\epsilon|n|}\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{{{0}}\}}\frac{\|\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{1+\beta}}\leq\bar{C}_{6\epsilon}(\omega),

(7.19)

which together with (
7.16
) and (
7.9
) gives

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\epsilon|n|}\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)e^{-\epsilon|n|}\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi)\|\,e^{-6\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{1+\beta}}\,e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-\epsilon|n|}\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{\kappa}(n,\varpi)\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi)\|\,\|D\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|\frac{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{1+\beta}_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)K_{\epsilon}(\omega)^{2}e^{\lambda_{\max}}\bar{C}_{6\epsilon}(\omega)+\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)e^{\lambda_{\max}}K_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n+1}\omega)C_{\lambda}\delta(\theta^{n}\omega)\,\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{9\epsilon}(\omega)+\frac{1}{2C_{\lambda}}\,\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\},

(7.20)

where
\hat{C}_{9\epsilon}(\omega):=\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)K_{\epsilon}(\omega)^{2}e^{\lambda_{\max}}\bar{C}_{6\epsilon}(\omega)>0
. In (
7.20
), we also use the first inequality of (
5.15
) as well as the facts that

\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi)\|\leq K_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n+1}\omega)e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}+\epsilon}\leq e^{\epsilon|n|}e^{\lambda_{\max}}K_{\epsilon}(\omega)

due to (
5.5
) with
-1
and
\vartheta^{n+1}\varpi
in place of
n
and
\varpi
respectively, and

\delta(\theta^{n}\omega)=1/\mathfrak{M}_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)\leq\{2e^{\lambda_{\max}}C_{\lambda}^{3}K_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)K_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n+1}\omega)\}^{-1}=\{2e^{\lambda_{\max}}C_{\lambda}^{2}\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n}\omega)K_{\epsilon}(\theta^{n+1}\omega)\}^{-1}

due to (
3.6
). Similarly, we have

e^{-6\epsilon|n|}\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{{{0}}\}}\frac{\|\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{\varsigma}}\leq\bar{C}_{6\epsilon}(\omega).

Therefore,

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{\varsigma}}\leq\hat{C}_{9\epsilon}(\omega)+\frac{1}{2C_{\lambda}}\,\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}.

(7.21)

Then, it is clear that

{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}

provided
\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}
. Furthermore, (
7.20
)-(
7.21
) imply that for
\psi_{0}:={\bf 0}\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}
(
{\bf 0}
is the zero mapping)

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{0}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\leq\hat{C}_{9\epsilon}(\omega),

(7.22)

and that for
\psi,\tilde{\psi}\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|({\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi-{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\tilde{\psi})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\leq\frac{1}{2C_{\lambda}}\,\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|(\psi-\tilde{\psi})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}.

(7.23)

Step 3
.
Recursive approximation of the solution
.

Now, we construct a sequence of mappings
\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}

by recursively defining

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot):={\bf 0}\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}\quad{\rm and}\quad\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot):=T_{i}^{-1}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{\ell-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot),\quad\forall\ell\in\mathbb{N}.

(7.24)

In what follows, we prove that the sequence
is well defined and convergent for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. In fact, it is clear that

\psi_{0}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)

satisfies

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\psi_{0}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\}={\bf 0}.

Then, we see from (
7.15
) and (
7.22
) that

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\psi_{1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|(T_{i}^{-1}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{0})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\leq C_{\lambda}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|({\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{0})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{C}_{9\epsilon}(\omega)

(7.25)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
It means that we obtain a mapping

\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)
with
\ell=1
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}{n}-10\epsilon|{n}|}\|\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\}<\infty.

(7.26)

Suppose that (
7.26
) holds for a certain
\ell\geq 1
.
Then, we see from (
7.20
)-(
7.21
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}{n}-10\epsilon|{n}|}\|{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\}\leq\hat{C}_{9\epsilon}(\omega)+\frac{1}{2C_{\lambda}}\,\sup_{{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}{{n}}-10\epsilon|{{n}}|}\|\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\}<\infty,

implying that
\psi_{\ell+1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot):=T_{i}^{-1}{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}
is well defined such that

\displaystyle\sup_{{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}{n}-10\epsilon|{n}|}\|\psi_{\ell+1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\}<\infty

by (
7.15
), i.e., (
7.26
) holds with
\ell
being replaced by
\ell+1
. Hence, by induction on
\ell
we conclude that
\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}
are well defined for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.

Next, in view of
(
7.15
) and (
7.23
), we compute that

\displaystyle\sup_{{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}{n}-10\epsilon|{n}|}\|(\psi_{\ell+1}-\psi_{\ell})(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}{n}-10\epsilon|{n}|}\big\|\big\{T_{i}^{-1}({\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{\ell}-{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{\ell-1})\big\}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\big\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\lambda}\sup_{{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}{n}-10\epsilon|{n}|}\big\|({\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{\ell}-{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\psi_{\ell-1})({n},\cdot)\big\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{{n}\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}{n}-10\epsilon|{n}|}\|(\psi_{\ell}-\psi_{\ell-1})({n},\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}.

(7.27)

This enables us to define

\displaystyle{N}_{\ell}:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|(\psi_{\ell}-\psi_{\ell-1})({n},\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\},\qquad\forall\ell\in\mathbb{N},

which satisfies that

{N}_{\ell+1}\leq(1/2){N}_{\ell}\leq(1/2)^{\ell}{N}_{1}.

Then

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\psi_{\ell+1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)-\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\leq{N}_{\ell+1}\leq(1/2)^{\ell}{N}_{1},

which implies that the limit

\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot):=\lim_{\ell\to+\infty}\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\{\psi_{\ell+1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)-\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\}

exists for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i}
and

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\psi_{\ell+1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)-\psi_{\ell}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}

\displaystyle\leq 2{N}_{1}=2\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\psi_{1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}\leq\check{M}_{v}(\omega),

(7.28)

where
\check{M}_{v}(\omega):=2\hat{C}_{9\epsilon}(\omega)
by (
7.25
).
This solves equation (
7.6
) such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-12\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{1+\beta}}\leq\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)^{2}e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\leq M_{v}(\omega),\\
&e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-12\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{\varsigma}}\leq\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)^{2}e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\leq M_{v}(\omega),\end{split}

(7.29)

where
M_{v}(\omega):=\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)^{2}\check{M}_{v}(\omega)>0
.

Hence, according to (
7.4
), we define

\displaystyle{\bar{\bf v}}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}):=\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{\kappa}(n,\varpi)\,\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}+\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}),\qquad\forall i=1,...,\tau,\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{Z},

which are the solutions of equations (
5.26
) for every
\varpi\in\bar{\bf\Omega}
such that
{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,{{0}})=\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{\kappa}(n,\varpi)\,\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}\in X_{\kappa}
and

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{{{0}}\}}\bigg\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-12\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})-{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,{{0}})\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{1+\beta}}\bigg\}\leq M_{v}(\omega)

(7.30)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{{{0}}\}}\bigg\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-12\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})-{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,{{0}})\|}{\|x_{i}\|^{\varsigma}}\bigg\}\leq M_{v}(\omega)

(7.31)

for
i=1,...,\tau
because of (
7.29
). Thus, we prove (
5.28
) by (
7.30
) (putting
n=0
) and prove (
5.29
) by (
7.31
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
5.2
.
\Box

Proof of Lemma
7.1
. First of all, notice that for a linear operator
T

if there are norms
\|\cdot\|_{1}
and
\|\cdot\|_{2}
such that
\|{\rm id}-T\|_{1}<1
and
\|({\rm id}-T)^{-1}\|_{2}<1
respectively, then

\displaystyle T^{-1}h=-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Big(({\rm id}-T)^{-k}h\Big)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(({\rm id}-T)^{k}h\Big)

(7.32)

by the uniqueness of the inverse.
We show that for any
\{h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}
satisfying (
7.13
) the linear operators
T_{i,j}
defined by (
7.7
) are
invertible such that
T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,{{0}})={{0}}
and

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}}\leq C_{\lambda}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}}\Big\}.

(7.33)

In fact, we compute from (
7.10
) that for
k\geq 0

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\big\|\big\{({\rm id}-T_{i,j})^{-k}h_{i,j}\big\}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\big\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{j}(k,\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi)h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}e^{(\lambda_{j}+\epsilon)k}\frac{\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}+11\epsilon)k}\,\frac{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}(n-k)-10\epsilon|n-k|}\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,j}}{\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\frac{\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}+11\epsilon)k}\,e^{-(\lambda_{i}-\epsilon)(1+\beta)k}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}(n-k)-10\epsilon|n-k|}\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n-k}_{i,j}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}-\beta\lambda_{i}+13\epsilon)k}\sup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}m-10\epsilon|m|}\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{m}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,m}_{i,j}}\Big\}.

(7.34)

Similarly, we compute that for
k\geq 0

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\big\|\big\{({\rm id}-T_{i,j})^{k}h_{i,j}\big\}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\big\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{j}(-k,\vartheta^{n+k}\varpi)h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n+k}\varpi,\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}^{1+\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}-\beta\lambda_{i}-13\epsilon)k}\sup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}m-10\epsilon|m|}\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{m}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,m}_{i,j}}\Big\}.

(7.35)

Notice that either

\displaystyle e^{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}-\beta\lambda_{i}+13\epsilon}\in(0,1)\quad{\rm or}\quad e^{-(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}-\beta\lambda_{i}-13\epsilon)}\in(0,1)

(7.36)

for
i=1,...,\tau
,
\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p}
,
j=1,...,{p}
by (
2.10
), (
3
) and (
(iii)
).
It follows that either the series

\displaystyle T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Big(({\rm id}-T_{i,j})^{-k}h_{i,j}\Big)(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})

(7.37)

or the series

\displaystyle T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(({\rm id}-T_{i,j})^{k}h_{i,j}\Big)(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})

(7.38)

converges such that

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}}\leq C_{\lambda}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}}\Big\},

which proves (
7.33
) with a constant
C_{\lambda}>0
.

Moreover, we show that

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\Upsilon^{\varsigma,n}_{i,j}}\leq C_{\lambda}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\varsigma,n}_{i,j}}\Big\}.

(7.39)

In fact, since (
3
) and (
5.30
) imply that either

e^{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\varsigma\lambda_{i}+12\epsilon}\in(0,1)\quad{\rm or}\quad e^{-(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\varsigma\lambda_{i}-12\epsilon)}\in(0,1),

by the same arguments as the previous paragraph we see that either of the series (
7.37
) or (
7.38
) converges such that (
7.39
) holds. Remark that for all
(i,\kappa,j)

it is possible that

\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}+13\epsilon<0<\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\varsigma\lambda_{i}+12\epsilon

since
\lambda_{i}>0
and
\varsigma\in(0,1)
. This fact requires us to use different equalities (
7.37
) and (
7.38
) to estimate

\|T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}}

and

\|T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\Upsilon^{\varsigma,n}_{i,j}}

separately, which is allowed because of (
7.32
).
Then, combining (
7.33
) with (
7.39
), we have
T_{i,j}^{-1}{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and (
7.14
) holds.
The proof of Lemma
7.1
is completed.
\Box

Remark that (
7.6
) is a thorough decomposition of equation (
5.17
) in every subspace
X_{i}
. This decomposition makes it possible to use (
7.36
), coming from (
2.10
), to guarantee the convergence of (
7.37
) or (
7.38
), which is the key step to solve (
7.6
). Otherwise, without the decomposition, we would need the bunching condition (
1.7
),
which is stronger than (
2.10
)), to
guarantee the convergence of some relevant sequences.

Moreover, the Lyapunov norm makes the two coefficients on both sides of (
7.14
) to be identical (equal to
e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}
). This is important to the recursive construction of the solutions of equations (
7.6
) (see (
7.24
)). Without the Lyapunov norm, the coefficient on the left hand side would be

e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-c\epsilon|n|}
with
c>10
, which would cause divergence of the sequence constructed in (
7.24
).

8.
Solving the decomposed cohomological equation when
\alpha=0

In this section, we solve equation (
5.26
), i.e., proving Proposition
5.2
, in the case of
\alpha=0
. Since the proof for (
5.29
) is exactly the same as the one given in section 7, we are mainly concerned with (
5.28
).

The main idea to prove (
5.28
) is almost the same as the one given in section 7, but the only difference is that we cannot use the norm
\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}}
, given in (
7.12
), because (
7.18
) cannot hold when
\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)
is only
C^{2}
. Thus, we need to use another
appropriate norm instead. In order to define the new norm, we first give the following lemma.

Lemma 8.1
.

For any constants
{\epsilon}>0
and
\lambda>0
, there is a non-decreasing function
\chi_{\varpi,i}:(0,+\infty)\to(0,+\infty)
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}}\frac{e^{-6\epsilon|n|}\|\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|}{\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|)}\leq 1,\\
&\lim_{s\to 0}\chi_{\varpi,i}(s)=0,\quad\chi_{\varpi,i}(e^{\lambda k}s)\leq e^{{\epsilon}k}\chi_{\varpi,i}(s),\quad\forall k\geq 0,~\forall s\in(0,+\infty).\end{split}

(8.1)

Proof
. Since we do not need the measurability of
\chi_{\varpi,i}
with respect to
\varpi
, we fix an arbitrary
\varpi\in\bar{\bf\Omega}
and therefore a constant means a function depending on
\varpi
in this proof (only).

Notice that
D^{2}\bar{\bf f}(\varpi,\cdot)
is
\epsilon
-slowly continuous, as mentioned just below (
5.15
), and that
D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\varpi,\cdot)
is
5\epsilon
-slowly continuous at
0
, as seen in (
5.20
) and (
5.24
). Thus,
e^{-\epsilon|n|}D^{2}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,{\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)})
and

e^{-5\epsilon|n|}D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)
are equicontinuous at
0
with respect to
n
for every
\varpi\in\bar{\bf\Omega}
. It follows that

\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot))D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)

is
6\epsilon
-slowly continuous at
0
.

Then, by (
7.17
), we see that for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
there is an
m_{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}
(independent of
n
and
x_{i}
) such that for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}

e^{-6\epsilon|n|}\|\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|\leq\hat{C}e^{-{\epsilon}\ell},\quad\forall\|x_{i}\|\leq e^{-m_{\ell}},

with a constant
\hat{C}>0
. In fact,
choosing sufficiently large
\hat{C}>0
, we put
m_{0}=0
.
Inductively,
when
m_{\ell}
is fixed, we choose a sufficiently lager
m_{\ell+1}
such that

\displaystyle m_{\ell+1}-m_{\ell}\geq\lambda,\qquad\forall\ell\geq 0.

(8.2)

Then, define a function
\chi_{\varpi,i}:(0,+\infty)\to(0,+\infty)
as

\displaystyle\chi_{\varpi,i}(s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}\hat{C}e^{-{\epsilon}\ell},&\forall s\in I_{\ell}:=(e^{-m_{\ell+1}},e^{-m_{\ell}}],&\forall\ell\geq 0,\\
\hat{C},&\forall s>1,\end{array}\right.

which satisfies (
8.1
). Clearly,
\chi_{\varpi,i}
is non-decreasing and the first two formulae of (
8.1
) hold.

Concerning the last formulae of (
8.1
), we assume that
\ell\geq 0
and
k\geq 0
are given. Then for any
s\in I_{\ell}
(implying
s\leq 1
) we have either

e^{\lambda k}s\leq 1
or
e^{\lambda k}s>1
. In the case of

e^{\lambda k}s\leq 1
, we see that
e^{\lambda k}s\in I_{\ell-\hat{k}}=(e^{-m_{\ell-\hat{k}+1}},e^{-m_{\ell-\hat{k}}}]
with an integer
\hat{k}\leq\ell
.
It follows that
\ell-\hat{k}
is the largest integer such that

\displaystyle e^{\lambda k}s\leq e^{-m_{\ell-\hat{k}}}.

On the other hand, when
k\leq\ell
, the fact that
s\in I_{\ell}
and (
8.2
) imply that

\displaystyle e^{\lambda k}s\leq e^{\lambda k-m_{\ell}}\leq e^{(m_{\ell}-m_{\ell-1})+\cdots+(m_{\ell-k+1}-m_{\ell-k})-m_{\ell}}\leq e^{-m_{\ell-k}}.

It means that

\ell-\hat{k}\geq\ell-k
, implying

\displaystyle\hat{k}\leq k,

(8.5)

When
k>\ell
,
(
8.5
) still holds true since
k>\ell\geq\hat{k}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\chi_{\varpi,i}(e^{\lambda k}s)=\hat{C}e^{-{\epsilon}\ell}e^{{\epsilon}\hat{k}}=e^{{\epsilon}\hat{k}}\chi_{\varpi,i}(s)\leq e^{{\epsilon}k}\chi_{\varpi,i}(s)

by (
8
), where
e^{\lambda k}s\in I_{\ell-\hat{k}}
and
s\in I_{\ell}
.

In the case of
e^{\lambda k}s>1
, we have
k>\ell
. Otherwise (i.e.,
k\leq\ell
),

\displaystyle e^{\lambda k}s\leq e^{\lambda\ell}s\leq e^{\lambda\ell-m_{\ell}}\leq e^{-m_{0}}=1

since
\lambda\ell\leq m_{\ell}-m_{0}
by (
8.2
), which contradicts the fact that
e^{\lambda k}s>1
. Thus,

k>\ell
implies that

\displaystyle\chi_{\varpi,i}(e^{\lambda k}s)=\hat{C}\,\leq\hat{C}\,e^{{\epsilon}k}e^{-{\epsilon}\ell}=e^{{\epsilon}k}\chi_{\varpi,i}(s)

by (
8
).
Since
\chi_{\varpi,i}(e^{\lambda k}s)\leq e^{{\epsilon}k}\chi_{\varpi,i}(s)
holds clearly for
s>1
, the last inequality of (
8.1
) is proved.
\qquad\Box

Proof of Proposition
5.2
. First of all, we define a new space

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{\chi,n}_{i,j}:=\bigg\{h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\in C^{0}(X_{i},X_{j}):h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,0)=0,\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{0\}}\frac{\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{n,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}<\infty\bigg\},

where
\chi_{\varpi,i}
is given in Lemma
8.1
, to replace

\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}
given just above (
7.12
), and define a new norm

\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,n}_{i,j}}:=\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{0\}}\frac{\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{n,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}

to replace

\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}}

given in (
7.12
).
Then,

\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}:=\Upsilon^{\chi,n}_{i,j}\cap\Upsilon^{\varsigma,n}_{i,j}

is a Banach space equipped with the norm

\displaystyle\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}}:=\max\big\{\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,n}_{i,j}},~\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\varsigma,n}_{i,j}}\big\}.

(8.6)

This enables us to define

\Upsilon^{n}_{i}:=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{{p}}\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j},

which is also a Banach space equipped with the norm

\|h_{i}\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}:=\max_{j=1,...,{p}}\|h_{i,j}\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i,j}}.

In order to prove (
7.28
), we observe that it suffices to replace
(
7.20
) and (
7.33
), both of which involve the norm
\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{1+\beta,n}_{i,j}}
, with new estimates involving the norm
\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,n}_{i,j}}
.

For (
7.20
), we first use the inequalities

\displaystyle e^{-6\epsilon|n|}\sup_{x_{i}\in X_{i}\backslash\{O\}}\frac{\|\partial_{x_{s}}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i}))\|}{\|x_{i}\|\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|)}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-6\epsilon|n|}\frac{\sup_{c\in(0,1)}\|\partial^{2}_{x_{s}x}\bar{\bf f}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,cx_{i}))D\bar{\varphi}_{i}^{-1}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)|_{cx_{i}}\|}{\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|cx_{i}\|)}\leq 1,

obtained from (
8.1
), to replace (
7.19
). Then the same argument as (
7.20
) gives

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{n,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}\leq\hat{C}_{9\epsilon}(\omega)+\frac{1}{2C_{\lambda}}\,\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}.

(8.7)

Concerning (
7.33
), we replace it by

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|T_{i,j}^{-1}h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,n}_{i,j}}\leq C_{\lambda}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|{h}_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,n}_{i,j}}\Big\}.

(8.8)

In fact, by (
7.10
) and (
8.1
)

\displaystyle\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(\pm k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n\pm k}\varpi,i})\leq\chi_{\varpi,i}(e^{(\lambda_{i}+\epsilon)k}\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})\leq e^{{\epsilon}k}\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
k\geq 0
and
x_{i}\in X_{i}
.
Then we compute from (
7.10
) that for
k\geq 0

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\big\|\big\{({\rm id}-T_{i,j})^{-k}h_{i,j}\big\}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\big\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\,\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{j}(k,\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi)h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{\bar{x},i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\,\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}+11\epsilon)k}\,\frac{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}(n-k)-10\epsilon|n-k|}\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,j}}{\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,i}\,\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,i})}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\frac{\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,i}\,\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(-k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,i})}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\,\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}+13\epsilon)k}\,e^{-(\lambda_{i}-\epsilon)k}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}(n-k)-10\epsilon|n-k|}\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n-k}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,n-k}_{i,j}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}+13\epsilon)k}\sup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}m-10\epsilon|m|}\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{m}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,m}_{i,j}}\Big\}.

Similarly, we compute that for
k\geq 0

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\big\|\big\{({\rm id}-T_{i,j})^{k}h_{i,j}\big\}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})\big\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{j}(-k,\vartheta^{n+k}\varpi)h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n+k},\bar{\bf\Lambda}_{i}(k,\vartheta^{n}\varpi)x_{i})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,j}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}-13\epsilon)k}\sup_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}m-10\epsilon|m|}\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{m}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,m}_{i,j}}\Big\}.

Notice that we have either

\displaystyle e^{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}+13\epsilon}\in(0,1)\quad{\rm or}\quad e^{-(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{\kappa}-\lambda_{i}-13\epsilon)}\in(0,1)

(8.9)

for all
i=1,...,\tau
,
\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p}
,
j=1,...,{p}
by (
2.10
) and (
3
). Then, using (
7.37
) and (
7.38
), we prove (
8.8
).

Other parts of the proof is exactly the same as the ones given in section 7, and therefore using (
8.7
) and (
8.8
) to replace (
7.20
) and (
7.33
) respectively, we get (
7.28
), i.e.,

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\leq\check{M}_{v}(\omega),

where we recall that
\|\cdot\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}:=\max\big\{\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\chi,n}_{i,j}},~\|h_{i,j}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{\varsigma,n}_{i,j}}\big\}

given in this section. Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{\|{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\varpi,x_{i})-{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\varpi,{{0}})\|}{\|x_{i}\|\,\chi_{\varpi,i}(\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\|x_{i}\|)}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\frac{\|{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\varpi,x_{i})-{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\varpi,{{0}})\|_{\varpi,i}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\varpi,i}\,\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\varpi,i})}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\bigg\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\frac{\|{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,x_{i})-{\bar{\bf v}_{i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,{{0}})\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}}{\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i}\,\chi_{\varpi,i}(\|x_{i}\|_{\vartheta^{n}\varpi,i})}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\sup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big\{e^{-\lambda_{\kappa}n-10\epsilon|n|}\|\eta_{i}(\vartheta^{n}\varpi,\cdot)\|_{\Upsilon^{n}_{i}}\big\}\leq M_{v}(\omega),

(8.10)

which proves (
5.28
) because
\chi_{\varpi,i}(\hat{K}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\|x_{i}\|)\to 0
for each
\varpi
(or
\omega
) as
\|x_{i}\|\to 0
by (
8.1
).
This completes the proof.
\Box

9.
Smoothness of the distribution

In this section, we prove the smoothness of distributions, namely, we will show the smoothness of
E_{s}(\omega,\cdot)
given in section 3 for each fixed
\omega\in\Omega
. By the remark given below Theorem
5.1
, it suffices to find a “canonical” basis
of
E_{s}(\omega,x)
independent of on
\bar{x},i
and give the relation between the “canonical” basis and the basis

\{{v_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{{p}},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}\quad{\mbox{for $x\in W_{i}(\omega,\bar{x})$ near $\bar{x}$},}

which is obtained in Theorem
5.1
. For this purpose, we need the following Journé’s lemma (
[
32
, Theorem]
or
[
34
, Theorem 3.3.1]
):

Journé’s lemma
.
Let
{\mathcal{W}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{W}}^{2}
be two transverse foliations of an open set
U\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
with uniformly
C^{n,\sigma}

(
n\geq 1
,
\sigma\in(0,1)
)
leaves. Suppose that a continuous function
v:U\to\mathbb{R}
is uniformly
C^{n,\sigma}
along each local leaf of
{\mathcal{W}}^{1}
and
{\mathcal{W}}^{2}
. Then
v
is
C^{n,\sigma}
on
U
.

Note that the
C^{1}
version of Journé’s lemma, i.e.,
n=1
and
\sigma=0
, also holds by
[
33
, Lemma 19.1.10]
. Moreover,
according to
[
34
, p.110]
), in the above lemma the notion “uniformly
C^{n,\sigma}
leaves”
means that those leaves vary continuously in the
C^{n,\sigma}
-topology and the continuity is uniform with respect to the base point. In view of Theorem
4.1
and Corollary
4.1
, we have that
all the foliations obtained in section 4 have uniformly
C^{1,\beta}
leaves on
U_{1}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|x\|<1/2\}
.

Moreover, the notion “a function
v(x)
is uniformly
C^{n,\sigma}
along each local leaf” means that
v
is
C^{n,\sigma}
along each leaf and all derivatives (up to order
n
) are uniformly bounded with respect to
x
and the leaf, and the
n
-th derivative along the leave is
\sigma
-Hölder with uniform Hölder coefficient with respect to
x
and the leaf.

We give the main result of this section as follows.

Theorem 9.1
.

Suppose that the random diffeomorphism
F:\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies
(
3.3
)

and that the Lyapunov exponents satisfy
(
2.10
)
.
Then
E_{s}(\omega,\cdot)
and
E_{u}(\omega,\cdot)
are
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, where

\displaystyle\beta_{\alpha}:=\min\{\beta,\beta_{v}\}=\min\{\beta,\beta_{E},\beta_{N}\}=\min\{\beta,\beta_{E},\epsilon/\lambda_{\max},\alpha\}.

(9.1)

Remind that
\beta\in(0,1]
is given in Theorem
4.1
and
\beta_{E}\in(0,1]
is given in Theorem
3.1
, both of which are determined by the Lyapunov exponents.

Proof
. In this proof,
we mainly study the smoothness of
E_{s}(\omega,x)
as the proof for
E_{u}(\omega,x)
is similar.

Step 1
.
Find the “canonical” basis
\{\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}
according to the solution of the L-P equation
.

First of all, we claim that

\displaystyle E_{s}(\omega,x)

\displaystyle=\{\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,z_{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:~z_{s}\in X_{s}\},\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(9.2)

with
q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})
being the first term of the sequence
(q_{n}(\omega,x,y_{s}))_{n\geq 0}
, which is a solution of the L-P equation (
4.3
) such that
q_{n}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)={{0}}
for all
n\geq 0
and
\sup_{n\geq 0}\{e^{-\varrho n}\|\partial_{y_{s}}q_{n}(\omega,x,y_{s})\|\}<\infty
for any constant
\varrho\in(\lambda_{\tau},\lambda_{\tau+1})
.
In fact, one can further verify that

F(n,\omega,x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s}))=F(n,\omega,x)+q_{n}(\omega,x,y_{s})

and therefore

\displaystyle DF(n,\omega,x)\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)=\frac{\partial F(n,\omega,x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s}))}{\partial y_{s}}\Big|_{y_{s}=\pi_{s}x}=\partial_{y_{s}}q_{n}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x).

Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 0}\{e^{-\varrho n}\|DF(n,\omega,x)\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,z_{s}\|\}\leq\sup_{n\geq 0}\{e^{-\varrho n}\|\partial_{y_{s}}q_{n}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\|\,\|z_{s}\|\}<\infty

and by the argument given just below (
5.46
) we have

\displaystyle\{\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,z_{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:~z_{s}\in X_{s}\}\subset E_{s}(\omega,x).

(9.3)

Since (
4.9
) indicates that every
\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}
is close to
\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}\in X_{\kappa,\iota}
,
we have that the linear space
\{\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,z_{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:~z_{s}\in X_{s}\}
has the same dimension as
E_{s}(\omega,x)
and therefore (
9.2
) is proved.

Hence, for every
\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p}
and
\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}
we define

\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x):=\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}\in E_{s}(\omega,x),\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

and see that
\{\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}

is a basis (independent of
\bar{x}
and
i
) of
E_{s}(\omega,x)
.
Next, in order to further study
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)
, we write for
\kappa=\tau+1,\cdots,p

X_{\kappa}=X_{\kappa,1}\oplus\cdots\oplus X_{\kappa,d_{\kappa}}\subset X_{s},\qquad x=x_{\kappa,1}+\cdots+x_{\kappa,d_{\kappa}},

where every
X_{\kappa,\iota}:=\{c\,\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:c\in\mathbb{R}\}
is a 1-dimensional subspace and
x_{\kappa,\iota}\in X_{\kappa,\iota}
, and let

\displaystyle\pi_{\kappa,\iota}x:=x_{\kappa,\iota}\in X_{\kappa,\iota},\quad\forall\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},\quad\forall\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}.

Then, for
y_{\kappa,\iota}:=\pi_{\kappa,\iota}y_{s}\in X_{s}
we compute that

\displaystyle\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}=\partial_{y_{\kappa,\iota}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{y_{\kappa,\iota}}(\pi_{s}q_{0})(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}+\partial_{y_{\kappa,\iota}}(\pi_{u}q_{0})(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}

\displaystyle=\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}+\partial_{y_{\kappa,\iota}}(\pi_{u}q_{0})(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\in X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u},

where we use
\pi_{s}q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})=y_{s}-\pi_{s}x
, as known in (
4.3
), and
therefore

\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\in E_{s}(\omega,x)\cap(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u}),\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

by (
9.3
).
Moreover, since

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y_{\kappa,\iota}}(\pi_{u}q_{0})(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\|=\|\partial_{y_{s}}(q_{0}-\pi_{s}q_{0})(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\|\leq\|\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)-{\rm id}_{s}\|\leq{\tilde{\delta}}(\omega)

by (
4.9
), we see that
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)
is close to
\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}
and

\|\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\|=\max\{\|\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\|,\|\partial_{y_{{\kappa,\iota}}}(\pi_{u}q_{0})(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\|\}=1,\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

for
\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p}
and
\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}
. Notice that

X_{s}+(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})=\mathbb{R}^{d}

and therefore

E_{s}(\omega,x)+(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})=\mathbb{R}^{d}

since
E_{s}(\omega,x)
is close to
X_{s}
(see (
3.14
)).
It follows that

\displaystyle{\rm dim}\{E_{s}(\omega,x)\cap(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})\}

\displaystyle={\rm dim}\{E_{s}(\omega,x)\}+{\rm dim}\{X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u}\}-{\rm dim}\{E_{s}(\omega,x)+(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})\}

\displaystyle=(d_{{\tau}+1}+\cdots+d_{p})+(d_{1}+\cdots+d_{{\tau}}+1)-d=1

and therefore
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)
is the unique unit vector in

E_{s}(\omega,x)\cap(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})

for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that is close to
\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}
.

Step 2
.
Give the relation between the “canonical” basis
\{\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}
and the basis

\{{v_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}
.

For the relation between the “canonical” basis
\{\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}
and

\{{v_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}\quad\mbox{for $x\in W_{i}(\omega,\bar{x})$ near $\bar{x}$},

we notice that if a vector
\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\neq{{0}}
in the 1-dimensional subspace
E_{s}(\omega,x)\cap(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})
satisfies that
\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)/\|\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\|
is close to
\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}
(the distance is
<1/2
), then
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)=\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)/\|\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\|
. Thus, we assume that

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)

\displaystyle:=\big(v_{\bar{x},i}^{{\tau}+1,1}(\omega,x),...,v_{\bar{x},i}^{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x),...,v_{\bar{x},i}^{{p},d_{p}}(\omega,x)\big)\big(-c_{{\tau}+1,1},...,1-c_{{\kappa,\iota}},...,-c_{{p},d_{p}}\big)^{T}

\displaystyle\in E_{s}(\omega,x)

and, at the same time, assume that

\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x):=\big(\vec{e}_{1,1},...,\vec{e}_{{\tau},d_{{\tau}}},\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\big)\big(c_{1,1},...,c_{{\tau},d_{{\tau}}},1\big)^{T}\in X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u}

for
c_{i,j_{i}}\in\mathbb{R}
, where the special forms of coefficients in the former equality (for examples,
-c_{{\tau}+1,1}
and
1-c_{{\kappa,\iota}}
) are just for the sake of convenient computation and
the coefficient
1
in the latter equality is used to make sure that
\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\neq 0
.

In order to determine all
c_{i,j_{i}}\in\mathbb{R}
, we consider linear algebraic equation

\displaystyle\big(v_{\bar{x},i}^{{\tau}+1,1}(\omega,x),...,v_{\bar{x},i}^{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x),...,v_{\bar{x},i}^{{p},d_{p}}(\omega,x)\big)\big(-c_{{\tau}+1,1},...,1-c_{{\kappa,\iota}},...,-c_{{p},d_{p}}\big)^{T}

\displaystyle=\big(\vec{e}_{1,1},...,\vec{e}_{{\tau},d_{{\tau}}},\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\big)\big(c_{1,1},...,c_{{\tau},d_{{\tau}}},1\big)^{T},

from which we find
\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\in E_{s}(\omega,x)\cap(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})
.
To solve the equation, we let

\displaystyle\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x):={v_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)-\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\forall\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\forall\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa},

(9.4)

which is small since
{v_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)
is close to
\vec{e}_{\kappa,\iota}
for any
x\in{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(\omega,\bar{x})
near
\bar{x}
. Then, putting

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\vec{c}:=(c_{1,1},...,c_{\tau,d_{\tau}},c_{\tau+1,1},...,c_{{p},d_{p}})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\\
&\Delta(\omega,x):=({\vec{0}},...,{\vec{0}},\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{{\tau}+1,1}(\omega,x),...,\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{{p},d_{p}}(\omega,x)),\end{split}

(9.5)

where
\vec{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the zero vector, we rewrite the above linear algebraic equation as

\displaystyle({\rm id}+\Delta(\omega,x))\,\vec{c}=\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x).

The smallness of
\Delta(\omega,x)
implies that the equation has a unique solution

\displaystyle\vec{c}\,(\omega,x)=({\rm id}+\Delta(\omega,x))^{-1}\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x),

(9.6)

which is also small since
\|\vec{c}\,(\omega,x)\|\leq 2\|\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)\|
.
Thus, we compute that

\displaystyle\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)

\displaystyle=\big(v_{\bar{x},i}^{{\tau}+1,1}(\omega,x),...,v_{\bar{x},i}^{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x),...,v_{\bar{x},i}^{{p},d_{p}}(\omega,x)\big)\big(-c_{{\tau}+1,1},...,1-c_{{\kappa,\iota}},...,-c_{{p},d_{p}}\big)^{T}

\displaystyle=\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}+\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)-\sum_{\hat{\kappa}={\tau}+1}^{{p}}\sum_{\hat{\iota}=1}^{d_{\hat{\kappa}}}c_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}(\omega,x){v_{\bar{x},i}^{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}(\omega,x)\in E_{s}(\omega,x)\cap(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})

and see that every
\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)
is
close to
\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}
and
\|\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\|
is close to
1
. Thus,

\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)/\|\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\|
is also close to

\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}
. According to the uniqueness of the unit vector
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(x)\in E_{s}(\omega,x)\cap(X_{{\kappa,\iota}}\oplus X_{u})
which is close to

\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}
, as mentioned at the end of the previous paragraph, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)=\frac{\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)}{\|\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\|}=\frac{\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}+\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)-\sum_{\hat{\kappa}={\tau}+1}^{{p}}\sum_{\hat{\iota}=1}^{d_{\hat{\kappa}}}c_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}(\omega,x){v_{\bar{x},i}^{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}(\omega,x)}{1+\tilde{\pi}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\big\{\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)-\sum_{\hat{\kappa}={\tau}+1}^{{p}}\sum_{\hat{\iota}=1}^{d_{\hat{\kappa}}}c_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}(\omega,x){v_{\bar{x},i}^{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}(\omega,x)\big\}},

(9.7)

where
\tilde{\pi}_{{\kappa,\iota}}z\in\mathbb{R}
denotes the
(d_{1}+\cdots+d_{\kappa-1}+\iota)
-th component of a vector
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\|\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\|:=\max_{\hat{\kappa}=1,...,p,\,\hat{\iota}=1,...,d_{\hat{\kappa}}}|\tilde{\pi}_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}\tilde{\zeta}_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)|
.

Now, we obtain the relation between the two bases

\{\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}\quad{\rm and}\quad\{{v_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}

for all
x\in{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(\omega,\bar{x})
near
\bar{x}
, where the former one
is independent of
\bar{x}
and
i
and is well defined in the whole space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Step 3
.
Show that
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,\cdot)
is uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{v}}
along the leaf of unstable foliation by the relation, obtained in step
2
, between two bases and by using the differentiability result of
v_{\bar{x},i}^{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)
, obtained in Theorem
5.1
.

In order to show that
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is differentiable at any point
\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
along the leaf
{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(\omega,\bar{x})
(
i=1,...,{\tau}
), for a small neighborhood
U\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
we define
\hat{U}:=U\times\cdots\times U
(the number of
U
is
d_{\tau+1}+\cdots+d_{p}
) and define a
C^{\infty}
mapping
G_{\kappa,\iota}:\hat{U}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
as

\displaystyle G_{\kappa,\iota}(\xi_{\tau+1,1},...\xi_{p,d_{p}}):=\frac{\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}+\xi_{\kappa,\iota}-\sum_{\hat{\kappa}={\tau}+1}^{{p}}\sum_{\hat{\iota}=1}^{d_{\hat{\kappa}}}\tilde{\pi}_{{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}\{({\rm id}+\Delta_{\xi})^{-1}\xi_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}\}(\xi_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}+\vec{e}_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}})}{1+\tilde{\pi}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\big\{\xi_{\kappa,\iota}-\sum_{\hat{\kappa}={\tau}+1}^{{p}}\sum_{\hat{\iota}=1}^{d_{\hat{\kappa}}}\tilde{\pi}_{{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}\{({\rm id}+\Delta_{\xi})^{-1}\xi_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}\}(\xi_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}+\vec{e}_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}})\big\}},

(9.8)

where
\Delta_{\xi}:=({\vec{0}},...,{\vec{0}},\xi_{\tau+1,1},...,\xi_{{p},d_{p}})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)=G_{\kappa,\iota}(\delta^{+}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x)),

(9.9)

where
\delta^{+}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x):=(\delta^{\tau+1,1}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x),...,\delta^{p,d_{p}}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x))
.
In fact, notice that, when
(\xi_{\tau+1,1},...\xi_{p,d_{p}})=\delta^{+}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x)
, we have, for
\hat{\kappa}={\tau}+1,...,{p}
and

\hat{\iota}=1,...,d_{\kappa}
,

\xi_{{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}+\vec{e}_{{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}=\delta^{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x)+\vec{e}_{{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}={v_{\bar{x},i}^{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}(\omega,x)

by (
9.4
), and

\Delta_{\xi}=({\vec{0}},...,{\vec{0}},\delta^{\tau+1,1}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x),...,\delta^{{p},d_{p}}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x))=\Delta(\omega,x)

by (
9.5
), which implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{\pi}_{{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}\{({\rm id}+\Delta_{\xi})^{-1}\xi_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}\}=\tilde{\pi}_{{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}}\{({\rm id}+\Delta(\omega,x))^{-1}\delta^{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x)\}=c_{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}(\omega,x)

by (
9.6
). Then, it follows from (
9.8
) and (
9.7
) that
(
9.9
) holds.

Next, recalling that
x_{i}\to x(x_{i})
is a mapping from
X_{i}
into
{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(\omega,\bar{x})
with
x({{0}})=\bar{x}
(given below (
5.34
)) and noting that
d\delta_{\bar{x},i}^{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}=dv_{\bar{x},i}^{\hat{\kappa},\hat{\iota}}
(see (
9.4
)) for all
\hat{\kappa}={\tau}+1,...,{p}
and
\hat{\iota}=1,...,d_{\kappa}
,
we see from (
9.9
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x(x_{i}))}{dx_{i}}\Big|_{x_{i}={{0}}}=DG_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\delta^{+}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,\bar{x}))\frac{dv_{\bar{x},i}^{+}(\omega,x(x_{i}))}{dx_{i}}\Big|_{x_{i}={{0}}},

(9.10)

where
v^{+}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x):=(v^{\tau+1,1}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x),...,v^{p,d_{p}}_{\bar{x},i}(\omega,x))
. By Theorem
5.1
and by similar arguments to the ones given below (
5.35
), we conclude that

\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)
is differentiable at
x=\bar{x}
along the leaf
{\mathcal{W}}_{i}(\omega,\bar{x})

with a
\beta_{v}
-Hölder continuous differential (with respect to
\bar{x}
), where the differential and the Hölder coefficient are uniformly bounded with respect to
\bar{x}
and
i
. Since
\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
can be chosen arbitrarily, we conclude that
\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,\cdot)

is uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{v}}
along the leaf for every
\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p}
and

\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}
.

Now, we show that
\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,\cdot)
is uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}

along every leaf
{\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,x)
of the unstable foliation, where
\beta_{\alpha}
is given in (
9.1
). This conclusion is clear when
\tau=1
since
{\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,x)={\mathcal{W}}_{1}(\omega,x)
due to (
4
) and (
4
). When
\tau\geq 2
,
by (
4.8
) we see a filtration of leaves, i.e.,

{\mathcal{W}}_{1}(\omega,x)={\mathcal{W}}_{\leq 1}^{uu}(\omega,x)\subset\cdots\subset{\mathcal{W}}_{\leq\tau-1}^{uu}(\omega,x)\subset{\mathcal{W}}_{\leq\tau}^{uu}(\omega,x):={\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,x),

and have that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\{{\mathcal{W}}_{\leq i}^{uu}(\omega,\check{x})\}_{\check{x}\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\leq i+1}^{uu}(\omega,x)}
and
\{{\mathcal{W}}_{i+1}(\omega,\check{x})\}_{\check{x}\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\leq i+1}^{uu}(\omega,x)}
(
i=1,...,\tau-1
) are transversal foliations (with uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
leaves by Corollary
4.1
) of

{\mathcal{W}}_{\leq i+1}^{uu}(\omega,x)~~~\mbox{(the leaf of a strong unstable foliation)},

which is a
C^{2,\alpha}
manifold and has uniformly bounded derivatives and Hölder coefficients with respect to
x
and
i
by Theorem
4.1
. Noting that straightening up this manifold by a
C^{2,\alpha}
transformation (whose derivative is
C^{1,\alpha}
) will keep the uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
smoothness of
\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,\cdot)
, we use the Journé’s lemma, given at the beginning of this section, on the manifold
{\mathcal{W}}_{\leq i+1}^{uu}(\omega,x)
. This proves inductively that
\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,\cdot)
is uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}

along the leaf
{\mathcal{W}}_{\leq i+1}^{uu}(\omega,x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
i=1,...,\tau-1
. Hence,
\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,\cdot)
is uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}

along the leaf
{\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,x)={\mathcal{W}}_{\leq\tau}^{uu}(\omega,x)
of the unstable foliation.

It
is worthy mentioning that although
[
32
, Theorem]
and
[
34
, Theorem 3.3.1]
only give the
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}

smoothness (without uniformity, as their whole space only has two transversal foliations) of the functions, we see that all constants in their proofs are determined by the bounds of derivatives (including their Hölder coefficients) of the leaves as well as the functions (along the leaves), which are all uniform with respect to
x
in our paper. Hence, all the above functions have uniform smoothness.

Step 4
.
Show that
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,\cdot)
is uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
along the leaf of stable foliation by using the classical result obtained by the L-P method
.

For the uniformly
C^{1,\beta}
smoothness of

\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)=\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}

with respect to
x
along each leaf of the stable foliation, we
fix an
\bar{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
arbitrarily. For any
x\in{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,\bar{x})
, let
H_{x}(\omega,\cdot):X_{s}\to{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)\,(={\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,\bar{x}))
be defined by

H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}):=x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s}),\quad\forall y_{s}\in X_{s},

which is a
C^{2,\alpha}
diffeomorphism by Theorem
4.1
.
Moreover, we assert that
H_{x}(\omega,y_{s})
and
q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})
satisfy the following properties:

(P1)

\pi_{s}H_{x}(\omega,y_{s})=\pi_{s}(x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s}))=y_{s}
;

(P2)

x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})=\tilde{x}+q_{0}(\omega,\tilde{x},y_{s})
when
\tilde{x}\in{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
.

In fact, we get from (
4.3
) with
n=0
that

\displaystyle q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})=y_{s}-\pi_{s}x-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Lambda_{u}({-k-1},\theta^{k+1}\omega)\,\Xi_{k}^{u}(q_{k})(\omega,x,y_{s}),

which implies that

\pi_{s}(x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s}))=y_{s}.

This proves (P1). For (P2), we notice from (
4.5
) that

x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})\in{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
. On the other hand, for any
\tilde{x}\in{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
we similarly have

\pi_{s}(\tilde{x}+q_{0}(\omega,\tilde{x},y_{s}))=y_{s},\qquad\tilde{x}+q_{0}(\omega,\tilde{x},y_{s})\in{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,\tilde{x})={\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x).

From the above discussion, we conclude that
x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})
and
\tilde{x}+q_{0}(\omega,\tilde{x},y_{s})

are two points on the same leaf
{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
, which have the same projection onto
X_{s}
, i.e.,
y_{s}
. Hence, we see that (P2) holds since, otherwise, the leaf
{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
cannot be Lipschitzian which contradicts (
4.9
).

Then we obtain from (P2) that

\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})=\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,\tilde{x},y_{s})

for all
\tilde{x}\in{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
,
which together with (P1) implies that

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}),\pi_{s}H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}))=\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}),y_{s})=\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s}).

(9.11)

Remind that the above
\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}),\pi_{s}H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}))

is the value of
\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})

at the point
(x,y_{s})=(H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}),\pi_{s}H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}))
.
Thus, using (
9.11
), the derivative of
\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,x)=\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}

at
x=H_{x}(\omega,\pi_{s}x)
along
{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,\bar{x})
(
={\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
) can be computed as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{d\zeta_{{\kappa,\iota}}(\omega,H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}))}{dy_{s}}\bigg|_{y_{s}=\pi_{s}x}

\displaystyle=\frac{d\big\{\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}),\pi_{s}H_{x}(\omega,y_{s}))\,\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\big\}}{dy_{s}}\bigg|_{y_{s}=\pi_{s}x}

\displaystyle=\frac{d\{\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})\,\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}\}}{dy_{s}}\bigg|_{y_{s}=\pi_{s}x}

\displaystyle=\partial^{2}_{y_{s}y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)\,\vec{e}_{{\kappa,\iota}}.

(9.12)

Since
x\mapsto\partial^{2}_{y_{s}y_{s}}q_{0,j}(\omega,x,y_{s})

is Hölder continuous with uniformly bounded Hölder coefficients with respect to
x,y_{s}
by Theorem
4.1
, employing a similar argument to (
4.24
),
we conclude that
\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,\cdot)
is uniformly
C^{1,\beta}
(and therefore
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
) along all leaves of the stable foliation because
\bar{x}

is chosen arbitrarily on
U_{1}
.

Now, we obtain that
\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,\cdot)
is
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
using Journé’s lemma again because
the leaves of stable and unstable foliations are transverse in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and uniformly
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
on
U_{1}
by Theorem
4.1
. Since
\{\zeta_{\kappa,\iota}(\omega,x)\}_{\kappa={\tau}+1,...,{p},~\iota=1,...,d_{\kappa}}
is a basis of

E_{s}(\omega,x)
, we see that

E_{s}(\omega,\cdot)
is
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
on
U_{1}
.

Finally, we extend the
smoothness of
E_{s}(\omega,\cdot)
from
U_{1}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|x\|<1/2\}
into
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
In fact, the following facts hold:

\bullet

V(\theta^{n}\omega)\subset U_{1}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
since
the diameter
\rho(\theta^{n}\omega)
of
V(\omega)
is
<1/4
(by (
3.6
)).

\bullet

Both
W_{1}:=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}F(n,\omega,U_{1})
and
W_{2}:=\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash W_{1}
are invariant under
F(n,\omega,\cdot)
.

Hence, we conclude that
F(n,\omega,\cdot)=\Lambda(n,\omega)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
on the invariant set
W_{2}
since
W_{2}\cap V(\theta^{n}\omega)=\emptyset
, implying that
F(\theta^{n}\omega,\cdot)=\Lambda(\theta^{n}\omega)
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
on
W_{2}
, as mentioned below (
3.3
). Then, by the uniqueness of the stable distribution (see the discussion for (
5.46
)),

\displaystyle E_{s}(\omega,x)=X_{s}\qquad\forall x\in W_{2}.

(9.13)

On the other hand, for any
x\in W_{1}
, we define

\displaystyle E_{s}(\omega,x):=\{DF(n,\omega,x)\}^{-1}E_{s}(\theta^{n}\omega,F(n,\omega,x))

(9.14)

with an integer
n
such that
F(n,\omega,x)\in U_{1}
. Notice that (
9.13
) still holds near the boundary of
W_{1}
. Thus,
combining (
9.13
) with (
9.14
), we obtain that
E_{s}(\omega,\cdot)
is
C^{1,\beta}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and,
similarly,
E_{u}(\omega,\cdot)
is
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
The proof of Theorem
9.1
is completed.
\Box

10.
Proof of the main result

Having those preparations in sections 3-8, we are now ready to prove our main result.

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.
We see from Theorem
9.1
that
E_{s}(\omega):=\{E_{s}(\omega,x)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}:x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
and
E_{u}(\omega):=\{E_{u}(\omega,x)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}:x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
are
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
distributions. Remind that we have already known that
E_{s}(\omega)
and
E_{u}(\omega)
are integrable by the existence of stable and unstable foliations and by (
9.2
).
Then, using the well-known Frobenius’ theorem (see e.g.
[
52
, p.541]
), for each
\omega\in\Omega
we obtain the
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
smoothness of random stable foliation
\{{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)\}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
and random unstable foliation
\{{\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,x)\}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}
.
Noting that
\pi_{s}\{x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})\}=y_{s}
and
\pi_{u}\{x+p_{0}(\omega,x,y_{u})\}=y_{u}
by (
4.3
) and (
4.2
), we get from
(
4.5
) that
\mathcal{W}_{s}(\omega,x)
and
\mathcal{W}_{u}(\omega,x)
are the graphs of mappings

\displaystyle y_{s}\mapsto\pi_{u}\{x+q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})\}\quad{\rm and}\quad y_{u}\mapsto\pi_{s}\{x+p_{0}(\omega,x,y_{u})\}

(10.1)

respectively, which implies that
q_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)

and
p_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)
are
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
for each
\omega\in\Omega
.

In this proof, the key step is to show the measurability of the first order derivatives of
q_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)

and
p_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)
with respect to
\omega
.
For the purpose,
one way is to note that the differential of the distribution along the leaf, obtained in Theorem
5.1
, is measurable. Then, we can modify the Journé’s Lemma and the
Frobenius’ theorem in the random case to show the measurability of the derivatives of foliations. However, we adopt another more straightforward method in the following.

First of all, straightening up the stable and unstable manifolds obtained in Theorem
4.1
by a tempered
C^{2,\alpha}
random differmorphism, we can assume that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,0)=X_{s}\quad{\rm and}\quad{\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,0)=X_{u}

(10.2)

at the very beginning of the proof, i.e., at the beginning of section 3.
Then, we define a random continuous mapping
\phi(\omega,\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
as

\displaystyle\phi(\omega,x):=x+\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,x,0)+\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,x,0),

(10.3)

and give the following lemma.

Lemma 10.1
.

The inverse
of
\phi(\omega,\cdot)
exists such that

\phi^{-1}(\omega,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

for each
\omega\in\Omega
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the unique fixed point of the mapping

\displaystyle x\mapsto z+\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}z,\pi_{u}x)+\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,\pi_{u}z,\pi_{s}x),

(10.4)

and that
\phi^{-1}(\omega,\cdot)
is also a random continuous mapping. Moreover,

\displaystyle\phi(\theta\omega,\cdot)\circ F(\omega,\cdot)\circ\phi^{-1}(\omega,\cdot)=F_{s}(\omega,\cdot)+F_{u}(\omega,\cdot),

(10.5)

where

F_{s}(\omega,\cdot):=\pi_{s}F(\omega,\cdot)|_{X_{s}}

and

F_{u}(\omega,\cdot):=\pi_{u}F(\omega,\cdot)|_{X_{u}}.

This lemma means that the random homeomorphism

\phi(\omega,\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},

defined in (
10.3
), decouples
F
into a contraction and an expansion. A similar result was given in
[
1
, Proposition 7.4.3]
, but here we construct
\phi^{-1}(\omega,\cdot)
via
p_{0}
and
q_{0}
.

Proof of Lemma
10.1
.
In order to prove that
\phi(\omega,\cdot)
is invertible, we first
observe that
the partial derivatives of
\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}z,\pi_{u}x)
and

\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,\pi_{u}z,\pi_{s}x)
with respect to
\pi_{u}x
and
\pi_{s}x
respectively are bounded by
1/2
due to (
4.9
), where

\displaystyle\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}z,\pi_{u}x)

\displaystyle=\pi_{s}z+p_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}z,\pi_{u}x)-\pi_{u}\{\pi_{s}z+p_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}z,\pi_{u}x)\}-\pi_{s}z

\displaystyle=p_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}z,\pi_{u}x)-\pi_{u}x

as known from the equality given below (
10.1
), and similarly

\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,\pi_{u}z,\pi_{s}x)=q_{0}(\omega,\pi_{u}z,\pi_{s}x)-\pi_{s}x
.
Thus, the unique fixed point can be obtained by the Banach Contraction Principle, and the fixed point is measurable with respect to
\omega
and continuous with respect to
z
.

Then, we recall from the proof of
[
42
, Theorem 3.8]
that
(p_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
or
(q_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
is the difference between trajectories with two initial points on the same unstable or stable leaf. Namely,

\displaystyle p_{0}(\omega,x,0)=\check{x}-x~{\rm with}~\check{x}\in X_{s}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,x),\qquad q_{0}(\omega,x,0)=\hat{x}-x~{\rm with}~\hat{x}\in X_{u}\cap{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)

(10.6)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and

\displaystyle p_{k}(\omega,x,0)=F(k,\omega,\check{x})-F(k,\omega,x),\qquad q_{k}(\omega,x,0)=F(k,\omega,\hat{x})-F(k,\omega,x)

(10.7)

for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. We also notice from (
4.5
) that

\displaystyle\check{x}+p_{0}(\omega,\check{x},\pi_{u}x)\in{\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,\check{x})={\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,x),\qquad\hat{x}+q_{0}(\omega,\hat{x},\pi_{s}x)\in{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,\hat{x})={\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x),

and therefore
\check{x}+p_{0}(\omega,\check{x},\pi_{u}x)=\hat{x}+q_{0}(\omega,\hat{x},\pi_{s}x)=x
by (
10.1
).

Hence, (
10.3
) and (
10.6
) mean that
\phi(\omega,x)=\check{x}+\hat{x}
, which is totally determined by the pair of leaves
{\mathcal{W}}_{u}(\omega,x)
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
,
and therefore
\phi(\omega,\cdot)
is one-to-one because different
x
corresponds to different pair of leaves, i.e.,
\phi(\omega,\cdot)
is invertible. Moreover, in view of (
10.3
)-(
10.4
) and (
10.6
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\phi^{-1}(\omega,\phi(\omega,x))

\displaystyle=\phi(\omega,x)+\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}\phi(\omega,x),\pi_{u}x)+\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,\pi_{u}\phi(\omega,x),\pi_{s}x)

\displaystyle=x+\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,x,0)+\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,x,0)+\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}(x+p_{0}(\omega,x,0)),\pi_{u}x)

\displaystyle\quad+\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,\pi_{u}(x+q_{0}(\omega,x,0)),\pi_{s}x)

\displaystyle=\pi_{s}(\check{x}+p_{0}(\omega,\check{x},\pi_{u}x))+\pi_{u}(\hat{x}+q_{0}(\omega,\hat{x},\pi_{s}x))=x,

which shows that

\phi^{-1}(\omega,z)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

for each
\omega\in\Omega
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the unique fixed point of the mapping (
10.4
).

Next, in order to prove (
10.5
),
we notice from (
10.7
) that

\displaystyle p_{k}(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)

\displaystyle=F(k,\theta\omega,F(\omega,\check{x}))-F(k,\theta\omega,F(\omega,x))

\displaystyle=F(k+1,\omega,\check{x})-F(k+1,\omega,x)=p_{k+1}(\omega,x,0),

(10.8)

and therefore the definition of
\Xi_{k}^{s}(p_{k})
given below (
4.2
) yields

\displaystyle\Xi_{k}^{s}(p_{k})(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)

\displaystyle=\pi_{s}f\big(\theta^{k+1}\omega,p_{k}(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)+F(k,\theta\omega,F(\omega,x))\big)-\pi_{s}f\big(\theta^{k+1}\omega,F(k,\theta\omega,F(\omega,x))\big)

\displaystyle=\pi_{s}f\big(\theta^{k+1}\omega,p_{k+1}(\omega,x,0)+F(k+1,\omega,x)\big)-\pi_{s}f\big(\theta^{k+1}\omega,F(k+1,\omega,x)\big)

\displaystyle=\Xi_{k+1}^{s}(p_{k+1})(\omega,x,0).

Similarly,
\Xi_{k}^{u}(q_{k})(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)=\Xi_{k+1}^{u}(q_{k+1})(\omega,x,0)
. Then, it follows from (
10.3
) and (
4.2
)-(
4.3
) that

\displaystyle\phi(\theta\omega,\cdot)

\displaystyle\circ F(\omega,x)=F(\omega,x)+\pi_{s}p_{0}(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)+\pi_{u}q_{0}(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)

\displaystyle=F(\omega,x)+\Lambda_{s}(\omega)\sum_{k=-\infty}^{-1}\Lambda_{s}({-k-2},\theta^{k+2}\omega)\,\Xi_{k}^{s}(p_{k})(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)

\displaystyle\quad-\Lambda_{u}(\omega)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Lambda_{u}({-k-2},\theta^{k+2}\omega)\,\Xi_{k}^{u}(q_{k})(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)

\displaystyle=F(\omega,x)+\Lambda_{s}(\omega)\sum_{k=-\infty}^{0}\Lambda_{s}({-k-1},\theta^{k+1}\omega)\,\Xi_{k-1}^{s}(p_{k-1})(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)

\displaystyle\quad-\Lambda_{u}(\omega)\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Lambda_{u}({-k-1},\theta^{k+1}\omega)\,\Xi_{k-1}^{u}(q_{k-1})(\theta\omega,F(\omega,x),0)

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\omega)x+\Lambda_{s}(\omega)\sum_{k=-\infty}^{-1}\Lambda_{s}({-k-1},\theta^{k+1}\omega)\,\Xi_{k}^{s}(p_{k})(\omega,x,0)+\pi_{s}f(\omega,x)+\Xi_{0}^{s}(p_{0})(\omega,x,0)

\displaystyle\quad-\Lambda_{u}(\omega)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Lambda_{u}({-k-1},\theta^{k+1}\omega)\,\Xi_{k}^{u}(q_{k})(\omega,x,0)+\pi_{u}f(\omega,x)+\Xi_{0}^{u}(q_{0})(\omega,x,0)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{s}(\omega)\pi_{s}x+\Lambda_{s}(\omega)\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,x,0)+\pi_{s}f(\omega,x+p_{0}(\omega,x,0))

\displaystyle\quad+\Lambda_{u}(\omega)\pi_{u}x+\Lambda_{u}(\omega)\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,x,0)+\pi_{u}f(\omega,x+q_{0}(\omega,x,0))

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{s}(\omega)\pi_{s}(x+p_{0}(\omega,x,0))+\pi_{s}f(\omega,\pi_{s}(x+p_{0}(\omega,x,0)))

\displaystyle\quad+\Lambda_{u}(\omega)\pi_{u}(x+q_{0}(\omega,x,0))+\pi_{u}f(\omega,\pi_{u}(x+q_{0}(\omega,x,0)))

\displaystyle=\pi_{s}F(\omega,\cdot)\circ\pi_{s}\phi(\omega,x)+\pi_{u}F(\omega,\cdot)\circ\pi_{u}\phi(\omega,x)=\{F_{s}(\omega,\cdot)+F_{u}(\omega,\cdot)\}\circ\phi(\omega,x),

where

x+p_{0}(\omega,x,0)=\pi_{s}(x+p_{0}(\omega,x,0))

and

x+q_{0}(\omega,x,0)=\pi_{u}(x+q_{0}(\omega,x,0))

due to (
10.6
). This proves (
10.5
)
and the proof of Lemma
10.1
is completed.

\Box

We continue to prove Theorem
2.1
. By Lemma
10.1
and the classical Implicit Function Theorem, for each
\omega

the smoothness of
\phi^{\pm 1}(\omega,\cdot)

is equivalent to the smoothness of
q_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)
and
p_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)
. Furthermore, the derivatives of
\phi^{\pm 1}(\omega,\cdot)
has the same measurability as the derivatives of
q_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)
and
p_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)
because the proof of the Implicit Function Theorem is essentially based on the Contraction Principle.

Notice that
q_{0}(\omega,x,y_{s})
is tempered
\sigma^{2}
-Hölder continuous with respect to
x
on
U_{1}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|x\|\leq 1/2\}
(see (
4.23
) in the case of
j=\tau
) and is tempered
C^{2,\alpha}
with respect to
y_{s}
(see Theorem
4.1
). Moreover, the domain on which
q_{0}(\omega,\cdot,y_{s})
is tempered
\sigma^{2}
-Hölder continuous can be extended from
U_{1}
to

U_{\phi}(\omega):=\{z=\phi(\omega,x):x\in U_{1}\}~{\rm with}~\|\pi_{u}z\|<1,

where
U_{\phi}(\omega)
is bounded by the constant
2
in view of (
10.3
) and (
4.9
) (noting that
p_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{u}x)=q_{0}(\omega,x,\pi_{s}x)=0
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\big\|\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,z,y_{s})-\partial_{y_{s}}q_{0}(\omega,\pi_{s}z,y_{s})\big\|\leq\tilde{L}_{\epsilon}(\omega)\|\pi_{u}z\|^{\sigma^{2}},\quad\forall z,y_{s}\in U_{\phi}(\omega).

Actually, in the proof of (
4.23
),
U_{1}
can be replaced with any bounded domain (see (
4.18
)) and the condition
\|x-\tilde{x}\|<1
is satisfied here, i.e.,
\|z-\pi_{s}z\|=\|\pi_{u}z\|<1
. A similar conclusion also holds for
p_{0}(\omega,x,y_{u})
. Hence, we have the following facts.

(B1)

The mapping
\phi(\omega,\cdot)
is a random homeomorphism such that

\displaystyle\|\phi(\omega,x)-\phi(\omega,\tilde{x})\|\leq L_{1}(\omega)\|x-\tilde{x}\|^{\hat{\sigma}},\quad\forall x,\tilde{x}\in U_{1},

(10.9)

with a random
variable
L_{1}(\omega)>0
tempered from above and a constant
\hat{\sigma}:=\sigma^{2}\in(0,1)
.

(B2)

For any
z\in U_{\phi}(\omega)
with
\|\pi_{u}z\|<1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\phi^{-1}(\omega,z)-\phi^{-1}(\omega,\pi_{s}z)\|\leq L_{2}(\omega)\|\pi_{u}z\|^{\hat{\sigma}}

(10.10)

with a random variable
L_{2}(\omega)>0
tempered from above.

(B3)

\phi(\omega,\cdot)
is a
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
diffeomophism since
q_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)

and
p_{0}(\omega,\cdot,\cdot)
are
C^{1,\beta_{\alpha}}
for every
\omega\in\Omega
, as mentioned below (
10.1
), but we do not know the measurability of
D\phi^{\pm 1}(\omega,x)
with respect to
\omega
.

In order to show that
D\phi^{\pm 1}(\omega,x)
are measurable with respect to
\omega
, observing (
10.3
), we need to prove that
\pi_{u}q_{0}(\omega,\cdot,0)
and
\pi_{s}p_{0}(\omega,\cdot,0)
have measurable derivatives. In what follows, we only deal with
q_{0}
and the proof for
p_{0}
is similar.

Regarding the measurability of the derivative of
q_{0}(\omega,\cdot,0)
, we consider the leaf
{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
of the stable foliation of
F(\omega,\cdot)
, and claim that
{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)
is flat when
\|\pi_{u}x\|>1/2
, i.e.,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{s}(\omega,x)=\{x+y_{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:y_{s}\in X_{s}\}=\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\pi_{u}z=\pi_{u}x\}\quad{\rm if}~\|\pi_{u}x\|>1/2.

(10.11)

In fact, using (
3.17
) in the case of
j=\tau
and
x=0
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Lambda_{u}(n,\omega)\pi_{u}x\|\geq(1/K_{\epsilon}(\omega))e^{(\lambda_{\tau}-2\epsilon)n}\|\pi_{u}x\|,\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(10.12)

which implies that
